The relaxed captain sipped her glass of bourbon and cracked a joke at Jack, who, as usual, laughed indignantly. The Italian, for her part, participated in the conversation while occasionally placing a discreet hand on her wife's thigh, a subtle but tender gesture. They hadn't had the long-awaited conversation about their future, but they had managed to ease tensions a little during their last meeting two days ago, when Maya had expressed her fears.

- "So, Carina," Travis teased, "the big day's coming up, isn't it? How are you going to celebrate?"

Carina had raised an eyebrow, confused.

-"the big day"

-"the divorce finalized, of course! Are you going to open a bottle of champagne or what?"

There was silence for a moment around the table, as if everyone suddenly realized the impact of the question. Maya had stopped drinking, her fingers clutching her glass slightly. As if the recent argument about moving in together hadn't been enough... Carina, for her part, had felt a hint of discomfort creep over her, but she had answered with controlled lightness.

- "I'm not sure it's really something to celebrate, but... why not." she said, a smile frozen on her lips.

-"I say we should have a fresh start party! With an official paper cake and everything!" joked Jack to break the tension.

Everyone had laughed, but the captain had said nothing, her gaze fixed on her glass. The doctor, despite her own efforts to brush off the discomfort, hadn't been able to stop herself from glancing over at her.

-"After all, you're getting divorced, but you're dating now..." added Vic, sensing the slight tension in her captain and friend.

The rest of the evening continued, but an imperceptible distance had settled between them. Even Carina's tender gestures - like her hand on Maya's shoulder, an exchanged smile - seemed a little less natural, as if something had cracked.

By the time they'd left the bar, the cool of the night had enveloped the group. The blonde had offered to walk the brunette to her car, as they had come separately after work, an excuse to get away from the others for a while.

Silence reigned for a few moments, disturbed only by the sound of their footsteps on the sidewalk. Finally, the Italian stopped and gently grabbed the blonde's hand, forcing her to do the same.

-"You were silent earlier..." she said softly, scanning her wife's face in the subdued light of a street lamp.

Maya had looked away, biting the inside of her cheek, a sign that she was searching for her words.

- "I don't like it when people talk about it like it's a party..." she finally says, her voice strained.

-"Me neither..." replied Carina. "But you didn't say anything. You haven't said a word. And that made me wonder... are you looking forward to this moment? To really... turn the page?"

The blonde had looked up, her gaze a mixture of surprise and pain.

-"What? No! Carina, no, of course not. Why would you think that?"

The Italian had let go of her hand, crossing her arms to protect herself from the cold and, perhaps, from the emotion that threatened to overwhelm her.

-"Because sometimes I feel like you're distant when I talk about our future... Like with our... Not moving in... Like you're not completely there with me."

Maya had run a hand through her hair, an annoyed sigh escaping her lips. That question about living together again.

-"I'm here. I'm completely here. But I'm not good at talking about my emotions, you know that. And sometimes it's... easier to make jokes or avoid the subject."

- "But you understand that it makes me doubt, don't you?" murmured Carina, her voice trembling.

The captain then approached, placing her hands on her wife's shoulders.

-"I understand. And I'm sorry if I gave you that impression. But this divorce... it's just a formality. How I feel about you is all that matters. And if I don't show you that enough, then I'll try to do better. Because you really mean a lot to me, baby."

The Sicilian's eyes misted over slightly, and she nodded before snuggling into Maya's arms. They stayed that way for a few moments, entwined in the cold of the night, until the captain murmured with a small smile:

"So we're not celebrating with a cake in the shape of official paper, are we?"

Carina had laughed softly, looking up at her.

- "No, but we could celebrate the fact that you've just opened up a little more to me."

-"I can do that..." replied Maya before kissing her tenderly, sealing their reconciliation under the mid-September stars. Maya may not have been ready to live with Carina, but neither did she seem eager to see their relationship come to an end, which was already a victory for the Italian.

After this reconciliation, the mood between them was a little more normal than it had been in recent days, but there was still a certain restraint on the obstetrician's side, as she had taken her girlfriend's questions rather badly. She knew it was all too fast, especially as they'd never said the three little words, but yet she'd thought it was more serious between them than it seemed to be for the firefighter. Had the blonde just wanted to put a name to their relationship because she couldn't bear to imagine « her wife » dating anyone else? Like some kind of property, or was she really attached to Carina as she was in return? The brunette wasn't the kind of woman to declare her love at the drop of a hat; she was more into gestures than declarations, but she wondered if Maya would ever be in love with her...

Two weeks after the recent events, they were at the American woman's place, on the sofa watching TV, but the program didn't appeal to the brunette, who had her head on her girlfriend's stomach, in a semi-sitting position, with her legs on the coffee table.

The blonde was playing with the little hairs behind Carina's ear, brushing her skin with each pass of her fingers. The doctor had turned, coming to look at her wife's face, her every feature.

After a few minutes, she had turned completely around and buried her face against her chest, feeling her heartbeat. Wrapping her arms around her body, she had closed her eyes.

The captain's hand had slid through her wife's hair to caress her scalp and her second hand had come to the small of her back. She had tilted her head slightly forward to breathe in her scent while closing her eyes.

Then the captain had looked at Carina, who was in her arms, eyes closed, seemingly in her favorite place in the world. She had looked at her for a moment before daring to move or even speak.

"You make my heart race..." Maya whispered after almost twenty minutes of embracing like this.

The Italian had opened her eyes and the way the blonde was looking at her had made her miss a beat. She had lifted herself slightly to come and kiss her, slipping one of her arms down the captain's back over her shoulder, then she had come to slide her face against her neck, before closing her eyes again.

-"Perché non riesci a capire quanto io sia pazzo di te? Sembra che tu tenga a me allo stesso modo eppure non sembriamo volere la stessa cosa... Io voglio te, per sempre..." [Why can't you see how crazy I am about you? You seem to care about me just as much and yet we don't seem to want the same thing... I want you, forever...]

-"love... I've made some progress in Italian, but not that much ..."

-"molto buono" [very good] she replied, as if to say «so much the better».

-"You... You're not going to tell me?"

-"I'm crazy about you... And your smell is my favorite in the world, just like your presence..."

- "and why «per sempre»?"

-"I'd like to stay in your arms like this forever..."

Maya had tightened the embrace of her arms around Carina's body and pressed the bottom of her face to the Italian's head, as if to tell her to stay there forever then.

Ten days later, the scene on the sofa already seemed distant, almost unreal, like a memory from another time. Since then, a latent tension had built up between them, fueled by heavy silences and awkward words.

That evening, as they shared dinner in a restaurant near the Italian woman's home, the conversation took an unexpected turn.

-"I think the whole thing has given us a chance to see how we work together," Maya had said, staring down from her wine glass with a faraway look in her eyes.

Carina, who had been enjoying her meal, raised her head in surprise.

- "What do you mean, « the whole thing »?" she'd asked, squinting slightly, not sure she understood the words to that, yet, woman.

The captain took a sip of wine before answering, a little nervously.

-"Well, this marriage, our relationship... I mean, it's kind of an experiment, right? To see if it works... We're good together, we decided to go out together... All this fake marriage has brought us to this and maybe one day we'll get married for real."

Maya bit her lip as she saw Carina's expression harden. She knew her words were clumsy, but they had crossed the barrier of her thoughts before she could hold them back. She was never comfortable talking about her emotions, let alone explaining how she really felt about her girlfriend.

The silence that followed was deafening. The Italian put down her fork slowly, her eyes darkening with incomprehension and disappointment. Each word had come down like an anvil on her heart, and she wondered if her companion was even weighing and thinking the words that had come out of her mouth.

- "One, an experiment?" she had repeated, her voice low but charged with emotion.

-"That's not what I meant," the captain had tried to say, realizing too late the significance of her words.

- "Then tell me what you meant, Maya, because that sounds exactly like what I understood."

The blonde had sighed, annoyed, her tone hardening.

-"Carina, we got married after a few hours and an absurd amount of alcohol. Of course I want it to work, but you can't ignore the fact that it started out on... unconventional grounds."

- "Unconventional?" had retorted Carina, her voice trembling slightly: "This marriage perhaps, but what we have now... it's not a game, Maya; it's not an «experiment». Not for me, anyway."

For a moment, Carina had wanted to add something, but words failed her. Was she being too demanding? Did she expect Maya to feel the same way she did, at the same time? But the wound was now too deep for her to contain her disappointment.

The firefighter, caught off guard, remained silent. She had preferred not to answer any further, fearing that she wouldn't use the right words, or that she wouldn't be able to make herself understood. She had the impression that their situation was reversed, that it was she who wasn't «English native» and couldn't find the right words. Or maybe it was fatigue.

She had decided to extend her hand to her wife.

-"you know what you mean to me Carina..."

-"I'm not sure... I thought I knew, but I'm afraid I was mistaken..."

A few days later, the Sicilian had managed to repress her anger until that evening with her colleagues, an event at which Maya had insisted on accompanying her despite recent events. Everything seemed to be going smoothly until the captain, in a clumsy attempt to be funny and get her wife's colleagues in her pocket, like Arizona, mentioned their impromptu wedding in Vegas.

-"It was truly a night to remember," she joked to a group of her wife's colleagues.

"A wedding worthy of a movie script: two strangers, a few too many drinks, and voila!"

The silence that followed was icy. Carina's colleagues had looked at each other, hesitating between laughing or changing the subject. The Italian had felt her heart clench, her embarrassment coupled with anger. They didn't know the whole story; indeed, she often evaded the question when asked how long they'd been together, or when they'd met; and this revelation, innocent as it seemed, had humiliated her.

The drive home had been freezing.

-"How could you say that in front of them? You know how much I value my privacy and my professional image!"

The blonde, visibly confused, had raised her hands in defense.

-"It was a joke, Carina. I didn't think you'd take it that way."

-"That's the problem, Maya. You never think. Not about me, not about how I feel," her wife had retorted, her voice trembling with anger.

"Did you really think it was the perfect time to share this?" the Italian had added, her voice breaking.

-"It was just a joke, Carina," the captain had replied, annoyed, not understanding why she was taking it that way, after all her colleagues had told her what it was all about.

"No one seemed to take it the wrong way."

-"No, of course not!" replied Carina, her voice trembling.

"Because they don't know it's true. Because they don't know that we're getting divorced and that we only started this marriage without knowing each other and even less that at the beginning of our so-called marriage, we couldn't even see each other without falling into bed with each other, over and over and over again! When we were nothing to each other!"

Maya had braked hard at a red light, her hands clenched on the steering wheel.

- "Maybe we should have stuck to that, then!" she'd blurted out, clearly at her wits' end.

-"To what? Nothing serious? Is that what you think, Maya? That I'm just a parenthesis in your life?" She'd said that before looking almost disgusted, her cheeks drowned in tears now: "I... I'm your experiment, aren't I?!"

The tone had escalated, each word driving a deeper wedge between them. Maya didn't respond, knowing that no matter what came out of her mouth, nothing would end well in this conversation. She stopped in front of Carina's house and put her hand on the key.

-"don't turn off the engine, you'd better go home tonight".

These two days had been unbearable. Every minute without a message, without a call, seemed to weigh down the silence between them. Maya had spent hours staring at her phone, hesitating to send a simple «I miss you», but she knew it wouldn't be enough, not after the words that had left her lips in the car; why did she have to be so scared?

For her part, Carina had alternated between anger and sadness, fighting the urge to make the first move. What was the point? The last conversation had broken them.

Two days after the argument, they had agreed that a break was necessary. Carina was the first to suggest it, her calm voice masking the underlying pain.

-"Maybe we'd better keep our distance, at least until court," she said, avoiding Maya's gaze.

The captain had nodded, her face closed, but her eyes betrayed a sadness she didn't want to show. In the end, she may have been brave in her profession, but in any case, she was a coward in her personal life.

They had parted that evening without a fuss, but the weight of their last exchanges weighed heavily on their shoulders. The brunette had watched the blonde leave her home, her life, wondering whether this was really the end, or just another trial they had to overcome.

Maya had driven away from the Italian, her hands clenched on the steering wheel. But no sooner had she left the apartment than an unbearable weight fell on her chest. She felt like turning back, knocking on her wife's door and telling her everything, without fear, without hesitation. That she'd never wanted their story to feel like a game, that every moment spent with her meant more than she could ever express. But her hands remained gripped to the steering wheel, her heart caught in a vice. She feared her words would only make things worse, and that retracing her steps would be just another misstep-after all, she'd never seen what true love should look like.

In the days that followed, the silence between them had been heavier and weightier than any past quarrel, with each absence of message or call deepening the emptiness between them. They'd gone from talking to each other every day to not talking at all.

In the weeks following their separation, Maya had immersed herself in her work, but the adrenalin hadn't been enough to soothe her inner turmoil. The barracks had become a refuge, a place where she could escape, for a moment, from the pain she felt. Yet even there, Carina's thoughts never left her. Every intervention seemed to be an excuse to escape reality, but deep down, she knew she couldn't avoid what was going on in her heart indefinitely.

During one particularly trying intervention, her frustrations and sadness had exploded. They were in the process of bringing a warehouse fire under control when, suddenly, a hose disconnected, sending a shower of cold water onto the already slippery floor. The moment had unsettled everyone. Maya had roared with frustration, and her firefighters had looked at her, surprised by the violence of her reaction.

- "Fuck!" She'd kicked the pipe to the ground with her foot, but the physical pain only reinforced her inner torment. She could no longer contain the agitation bubbling up inside her.

"I can't take it anymore..." Her hands had trembled, but it wasn't fear that paralyzed her. It was helplessness in the face of the situation, this life she'd let slip away, this love she'd broken without even knowing how. Just out of habit, out of knowledge.

Andy, who had been watching her intently for a few moments, had motioned to Jack to take over, and she had slowly approached her best friend. Her tone was calm, almost firm, but still protective. Putting her hand against her elbow or dragging her outside.

-"Do you want to talk about it?"

Maya had shaken her head, as if this question were an additional burden.

"I'm screwing up my life, Andy," she'd replied, her throat constricted. She'd almost looked down, unable to bear the force of her own words.

"I can't stop thinking about Carina. We hate each other but it's like I can't get her off my mind." Her eyes had filled with tears, which she had quickly wiped away with a wave of her hand.

-"I don't think she hates you" had begun the Puerto Rican, but the blonde hadn't let her finish.

-"I'm the one who screwed everything up. I can't even save my own relationships, so how can I save lives here?

I didn't know how to be a good best friend to you and now look what I've done with..."

She had fallen silent, anguish gnawing at her from within. The sound of the fire, the crushing heat, now seemed far away, as if it no longer mattered.

Andy, who had clearly understood the depth of her friend's distress, had shrugged and searched for words, no doubt to reassure her, but also to tell her what she didn't want to hear.

-"Maya, you're not here to save anything right now. You're here to save yourself. And you know how you feel, even if you don't want to admit it... You're in love with her."

Maya had shaken her head, disgusted with her own vulnerability. She wasn't ready to accept it, not yet. But Andy, impassive, had been right. Those words, painful as they were, had echoed a truth she could no longer ignore. She knew, deep down, that it was the truth. But the weight of that truth paralyzed her even more, like a burden far too heavy to bear. She didn't know if she was ready to face the intensity of her feelings for Carina, or if it even made sense anymore.

The Italian, for her part, had spent entire days managing her patients, accompanying women who were about to give birth, one consultation, one diagnosis, one advice after another. But despite the satisfaction of helping to create life, a persistent emptiness gnawed at her. She had often found herself alone with her thoughts, and that morning she hadn't been able to avoid facing her own emotional wounds.

As the couple sat in front of her, a blonde and a brunette, smiling, hands interlaced, their impatience palpable at the thought of their baby's arrival, Carina felt a pang of pain in her heart. They were so happy, so innocent in their joy, and here she was, having to guide them, give them advice on how to maintain an active sex life while preserving their pregnancy. Her words came out automatically, like a mask, but deep down, she could barely hear them.

Her thoughts quickly turned to her wife. How could she not? She remembered the strength of the carnal bond they'd shared, a bond she'd never known with anyone else. That insatiable desire, that passion that had united them in moments of pure complicity. It now seemed so distant, almost unreal.

Talking to two women, who in a way seemed to represent them, about maintaining intimacy was probably one of the worst tortures of her job in relation to her own personal situation; seeing happy couples, happy and in love, all the time, while her own life was going to hell, her life was going to hell, was unbearably painful, and yet she had to face up to it and put on a brave face.

Ma Maya... [But Maya...]

The thought of her had crept into her mind, invasive. Then, suddenly, she remembered a conversation they'd had when they first met. Maya had told her, in a firm voice, that she didn't want a child. It was a shock to Carina. She had always dreamed of having a family, of starting a home.

Ma come può Maya, la persona che... Che amo, non vuole? [But how can Maya, the person that... That I love, not want that?]

This confrontation, that of her feelings, raw and sincere, had hurt her.

She had tried to push the thought away, to tell herself that maybe Maya just wasn't right for her. She had wanted to convince herself that such a difference in desire, in outlook on life, was insurmountable. But the more she tried to convince herself of this, the more devastated she felt. She couldn't help it. She couldn't forget Maya. Her wife was under her skin.

Devo togliermela dalla testa. Presto il nostro divorzio sarà definitivo e non avremo più nulla da fare l'uno nella vita dell'altra.... Comunque, non è che voglia una vita con me, l'ha detto chiaramente... [I have to get her out of my mind. Soon our divorce will be finalized and we'll have nothing more to do in each other's lives... Anyway, it's not as if she wants a life with me, she's made that clear...]

The consultation was finally over. The couple, as cheerful as ever, had stood up, looking enthusiastic. They thanked her and left, the door closing on them with a burst of happiness that seemed so far away to the brunette. She remained there, alone in her study, looking heavy and tired. Once the door had closed, she had let herself fall back in her armchair, her head in her hands. The tears had come without warning, hot and uncontrollable. She didn't hold back, letting them flow silently, like a wave she couldn't stop.

-"Come sono arrivato qui?" she asked herself, her inner voice trembling. [How do I get here ?]

Volevo finalmente una stabilità. I want a life built on solid foundations. Ma è con Maya che mi sento completo. Perché è così difficile da accettare? [I wanted stability at last. I wanted a life built on solid foundations. But it's with Maya that I feel complete. Why is it so difficult to accept?]

She had let her thoughts swirl endlessly in her head. She found no comfort in this silence. The tears still flowed, but no answer, no solution seemed to be on the horizon. All she knew was that her heart was stuck somewhere, frozen in a past where anything was still possible. But there was no going back to that past. All she had left was the pain of a love she couldn't forget.

Maya had received the summons for the court appointment concerning the dissolution of their marriage, scheduled for the twenty-fifth of November, in just over a fortnight's time. Since the announcement, she had shut herself up in a kind of torpor, drowning in her work, empty-eyed, taking refuge in action so as not to have to face the consequences of what was happening. She had clung to her missions, to her interventions, like a lifeline, hoping that adrenalin and fatigue would eventually drown out the pain rising inside her.

Andy had watched her for days, silent but worried, seeing that her best friend was running away; since the Italian, even the old Maya no longer existed, as if hurricane Carina had swept everything away in its path. She'd seen her lose herself, detach herself little by little from everything around her, and she'd finally had enough. In their conversation that day, Andy hadn't pulled any punches. She was straight to the point, her tone dry and direct.

-"You're an asshole, Maya. A real cunt."

The captain froze, stunned by the brutality of her words. She knew it had come from her friend, but the pain of those few words had pierced her, as if she'd suddenly come face to face with the truth she'd been avoiding for too long.

- "What are you talking about?" she had asked, her voice tired, her eyes full of weariness. "I don't have time for your judgments."

Andy had looked at her intensely, unperturbed. She'd seen that her best friend was still running from the truth, but this time she had no intention of letting it slip.

-"You're in love with her, Maya! It's as simple as that. But you're still scared, so you want to keep control, push away everything you feel. What's wrong with you?!... I can't believe that after that eloquent speech you made at my wedding, you can act like this... I thought I'd given you faith in love, I don't see what you're blaming on your history, yes you've done things backwards, but so what?! Maya, you're madly in love with Carina... And she's just as crazy about you..."

The lieutenant's words had struck the American like a bolt of lightning. Every syllable seemed to echo in her head, bringing her to a realization she'd never wanted. She knew it was true, that everything she felt for Carina was stronger than she was, but she couldn't take the step, couldn't free herself from the fear that paralyzed her.

-"I don't even know how to fix it, Andy..." she'd replied, her voice trembling with despair. A gleam of confusion and sadness had crossed her eyes.

"It's... complicated. Too much has happened... We've both gone too far with our words; she's been awful, I've been awful....

Carina wanted a stable relationship... And I didn't know how to express to her that we were on the same wavelength... But with everything that's happened, I... I don't even know what I want anymore... and I'm afraid she doesn't want me anymore... She thought I was «perfect» and now she's seen my true face..."

She had looked down, exhausted, feeling lost in a whirlwind of contradictory thoughts. She had lost her way, felt trapped in her doubts and fears, unable to find her way out.

"I don't know how to... Love. Before Carina I'd never been in love... I grew up in a home where I didn't have that as a model, nor did I evolve in a family setting where I could watch love stories and see how we were supposed to behave..."

Andy, for her part, had been watching Maya, a firm, resolute expression on her face. She knew that her friend was locking herself up in her hesitations and pride, hiding behind her fears and doubts to avoid facing reality. She decided it was time to give her best friend a good shake.

-"there's no right or wrong formula... But you know that, Maya. If you want this to work, you've got to get moving. Hiding behind your work, or the lack of an ideal family role model, isn't going to fix this. Go get her before she leaves for good."

-"As soon as I open my mouth to her, everything comes out wrong since Kate asked us if we were going to move in together. It's as if my heart and brain want to be sincere with Carina, want to tell her what I want and how I feel, but my mouth is doing the opposite because... Because... She could break my heart" said the captain, tears welling up inside her at the harsh reality of what she was finally daring to say out loud.

-"So you'd rather break both your hearts?"

Maya you're my best friend and I love you, but you're handicapped when it comes to love; you've never been in love before Carina, you've never been in a relationship before Carina and yet here you are, marrying this woman, after only a few hours and... And from the very first second it's been impossible for you to stay away or just be friends... Maya, what if Carina was the one? What if she was... The love of your life, the one, the great, the only?"

Andy's words had struck Maya like a thunderclap. She didn't respond immediately, content to let her friend's words resonate within her. The silence between them stretched out, heavy with everything the blonde couldn't say, everything she couldn't understand. But deep inside her, something had shifted. A shiver of realization, like a glimmer in the darkness. Maybe Andy was right. Maybe there was still time, maybe she could still fix what she'd broken. But how? She didn't know. All she knew was that her heart no longer belonged to her, it belonged to Carina. And if she waited too long, she risked losing it for good. Her best friend's words now vibrated inside her: «what if Carina was the one? What if she was... The love of your life, the one, the great, the only?»

The Italian hadn't yet found peace with everything that had happened. She still felt overwhelmed by events, by the weight of the choices she'd made, including asking Maya for a break, and all this had intensified when she'd received the letter from the court regarding the dissolution of their marriage. The paper, cold and impersonal, had made everything far too real, far too brutal.

She couldn't ignore it any longer. Everything she'd been through with Maya, every moment, every decision, had come back to bite her in the ass. At one point, she'd thought it was all part of a bad dream, but when she received that letter, she'd realized it wasn't. This was reality, this was the end.

When Andrea, her brother, had asked her why she seemed so upset, the tension in her eyes hadn't escaped her . He'd been watching her with concern, but hadn't yet perceived the full extent of her feelings. Carina, exhausted, had let herself go, breaking the heavy silence that had weighed on her for too long. She had decided to talk to him, to open her heart to him, even if it tore her apart.

/Conversation in Italian/

-"You know, Andrea, the irony always catches up with us, I've spent so much time, so many relationships, not managing to invest myself fully, not managing to go beyond those famous three months, and today... Today I was ready, finally, and it's the woman I love who's not..." She had closed her eyes feeling her tears roll down her cheeks. She had opened her eyes again to continue.

"I knew I shouldn't fall in love with Maya... She's never had a serious relationship, she's only had one-night stands... And what about me? I thought I could change her? I thought she could be in love for once, have a real relationship and I... I thought I was ready..." She had raised one of her hands and wiped her cheek, under one of her eyes with a gesture of her fingers.

She had swallowed, the words heavy and difficult to pronounce. She felt an immense weight in her chest, a throbbing pain that wouldn't leave her. Carina knew that what she had just said was the truth, but that didn't reassure her. She'd come to the conclusion that everything she'd wanted so far - a life without attachments, without emotional dependence - no longer matched what she felt deep down inside. She had changed, without even realizing it.

Andrew, arms folded, had looked at her in bewilderment, almost incredulity. He didn't fully understand what was tormenting her, but he knew something was deeply broken inside her.

-"Ready? For what? For her?"

Andrew's question had sounded like an echo, and Carina had turned to him, a bitter smile on her lips. She had never imagined finding herself in such a situation, at this time in her life, but yet, here she was, admitting a truth she hadn't wanted to see earlier.

-"Yes, for her. And I hate it. Because every day I realize that maybe we weren't meant to be together, but I fell in love with her anyway and I thought maybe I'd fallen in love for the last time in my life..." She had paused, her eyes fading for a moment under the weight of her words.

"I thought I was the woman who couldn't make a long-term commitment, but she turned me upside down... Mamma always told me «Zita sempri, maritata mai»... With Maya, I know that I could have kept that promise, every day with her I felt submerged again, as if... as if every day I fell in love again for the first time, every second spent in her presence was exceptional, I'd never experienced anything like it, even lying on the sofa in her arms was unprecedented. I had the feeling that Maya was the kind of woman to show you every day what you meant to her, that's the effect she had on me for over four months, every day was different and she never took me for granted. I know she was afraid of Kate's question that day; but I feel like our lives exploded from that day on...

It's to her that I should have said « Zita sempri, maritata mai », to make her understand that even married or even just dating me, it would never lock her up like she could see her mother being locked up with her father... That's not what I want and that's not how our relationship was, we were pulling each other up and I just feel that now we've let go of each other's hands and that the fall is infinite, painful...
